 "short_description": {
  "id": "Little People of the Pryor Mountains (Nirumbee atau Awwakkulé) adalah ras kurcaci gaib dalam cerita rakyat Suku Crow yang tinggal di Pryor Mountains, Montana, kuat, ditakuti, sekaligus pemandu spiritual.",
  "en": "The Little People of the Pryor Mountains (Nirumbee or Awwakkulé) are a race of supernatural dwarfs in the folklore of the Crow Tribe who live in the Pryor Mountains of Montana, strong and feared yet also spiritual guides.",

  {
   "id": "Dalam cerita rakyat Suku Crow, Little People of the Pryor Mountains, disebut Nirumbee atau Awwakkulé dalam bahasa Crow, adalah ras kurcaci yang ganas. Mereka tinggal di Pryor Mountains di Montana dan karena itu pegunungan tersebut suci bagi Crow. Sumber akademik menggambarkan mereka sebagai kurcaci yang sekaligus manusia dan makhluk gaib serta dapat menjadi pemandu spiritual.",
   "en": "Crow Tribe folklore tells of the Little People of the Pryor Mountains, whom the Crow language calls Nirumbee or Awwakkulé, a fierce race of dwarfs. They live in the Pryor Mountains of Montana, which is why the mountains are sacred to the Crow. An academic source describes them as dwarves that are both human and supernatural and can act as spiritual guides.",
   "claim_ids": [

Mereka dikenal mencuri anak, makanan, obat, dan tembakau, dan dipercaya membuat mata panah batu.",
   "en": "The first source says they are no more than 18 inches tall, very strong, with short arms and legs and little or no neck.  "claims": [
  {
   "id": "little-people-of-the-pryor-mountains-c01",
   "source_id": "little-people-of-the-pryor-mountains-s1",
   "quote": "The Little People of the Pryor Mountains (known as Nirumbee or Awwakkulé in the Crow language) are a race of ferocious dwarfs in the folklore of the Crow Tribe, a Native American tribe.",
   "locator": "Intro",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Little People of the Pryor Mountains (disebut Nirumbee atau Awwakkulé dalam bahasa Crow) adalah ras kurcaci (dwarfs) ganas dalam cerita rakyat Suku Crow, suku asli Amerika.",
    "en": "The Little People of the Pryor Mountains (known as Nirumbee or Awwakkulé in the Crow language) are a race of ferocious dwarfs in the folklore of the Crow Tribe, a Native American tribe."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "little-people-of-the-pryor-mountains-c02",
   "source_id": "little-people-of-the-pryor-mountains-s1",
   "quote": "live in the Pryor Mountains, a small mountain range in Carbon County, Montana and Bighorn County, Montana.",
   "locator": "The Little People of the Pryor Mountains",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Menurut cerita rakyat Crow mereka tinggal di Pryor Mountains, pegunungan kecil di Carbon County dan Bighorn County, Montana.",
    "en": "According to Crow folklore they live in the Pryor Mountains, a small mountain range in Carbon County and Bighorn County, Montana."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "little-people-of-the-pryor-mountains-c03",
   "source_id": "little-people-of-the-pryor-mountains-s1",
   "quote": "Petroglyphs on rocks in the mountains, the Crow said, were made by these demon-like creatures. Because the Little People live there, the mountains are sacred to the Crow.",
   "locator": "The Little People of the Pryor Mountains",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Orang Crow mengatakan petroglif di bebatuan pegunungan itu dibuat oleh makhluk mirip iblis ini, dan karena mereka tinggal di sana, pegunungan itu suci bagi Crow.",
    "en": "The Crow said petroglyphs on rocks in the mountains were made by these demon-like creatures, and because the Little People live there the mountains are sacred to the Crow."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "little-people-of-the-pryor-mountains-c04",
   "source_id": "little-people-of-the-pryor-mountains-s1",
   "quote": "The Pryor Mountains Little People were also known for stealing children, food, medicine, and tobacco.",
   "locator": "The Little People of the Pryor Mountains",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Little People Pryor Mountains juga dikenal mencuri anak, makanan, obat, dan tembakau.",
    "en": "The Pryor Mountains Little People were also known for stealing children, food, medicine and tobacco."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "little-people-of-the-pryor-mountains-c05",
   "source_id": "little-people-of-the-pryor-mountains-s1",
   "quote": "The Crow also believed that the Little People created stone arrowheads, for the Crow themselves only knew how to make bone arrowheads.",
   "locator": "The Little People of the Pryor Mountains",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Orang Crow juga percaya Little People membuat mata panah batu, karena Crow sendiri hanya bisa membuat mata panah tulang.",
    "en": "The Crow also believed the Little People made stone arrowheads, since the Crow themselves knew only how to make bone arrowheads."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "little-people-of-the-pryor-mountains-c06",
   "source_id": "little-people-of-the-pryor-mountains-s1",
   "quote": "Generally speaking, the Crow would refuse to enter the Pryor Mountains due to their belief in the Little People.",
   "locator": "The Little People of the Pryor Mountains",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Pada umumnya orang Crow enggan memasuki Pryor Mountains karena kepercayaan mereka pada Little People.",
    "en": "Generally the Crow would refuse to enter the Pryor Mountains because of their belief in the Little People."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "little-people-of-the-pryor-mountains-c07",
   "source_id": "little-people-of-the-pryor-mountains-s1",
   "quote": "However, other kinds of offerings, such as beads, cloth, or tobacco, could also be left in order to win safe passage through the mountains.",
   "locator": "The Little People of the Pryor Mountains",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Persembahan lain seperti manik, kain, atau tembakau juga bisa ditinggalkan agar mendapat jalan aman melewati pegunungan itu.",
    "en": "Other offerings such as beads, cloth or tobacco could also be left to win safe passage through the mountains."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "little-people-of-the-pryor-mountains-c08",
   "source_id": "little-people-of-the-pryor-mountains-s1",
   "quote": "The Little People also were integral to the Crow practice of the Sun Dance.",
   "locator": "The Little People of the Pryor Mountains",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Little People juga merupakan bagian penting dari praktik Sun Dance suku Crow.",
    "en": "The Little People were also integral to the Crow practice of the Sun Dance."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "little-people-of-the-pryor-mountains-c09",
   "source_id": "little-people-of-the-pryor-mountains-s1",
   "quote": "The Little People are said to be no more than 18 inches (46 cm) (or knee) high.",
   "locator": "The Little People of the Pryor Mountains",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Little People dikatakan tidak lebih tinggi dari 18 inci (46 cm), atau setinggi lutut.",
    "en": "The Little People are said to be no more than 18 inches (46 cm), or knee, high."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "little-people-of-the-pryor-mountains-c10",
   "source_id": "little-people-of-the-pryor-mountains-s1",
   "quote": "and incredibly strong but short arms and legs; and little or no neck.",
   "locator": "The Little People of the Pryor Mountains",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Mereka digambarkan sangat kuat tetapi berlengan dan berkaki pendek, dan nyaris tanpa leher.",
    "en": "They are described as incredibly strong but with short arms and legs and little or no neck."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "little-people-of-the-pryor-mountains-c11",
   "source_id": "little-people-of-the-pryor-mountains-s1",
   "quote": "He also prophesied that Plenty Coups would become chief of his people, if he used his wits",
   "locator": "Role of Little People in the dreams of Plenty Coups",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Dalam penglihatan, kepala Little People juga menubuatkan bahwa Plenty Coups akan menjadi kepala suku jika menggunakan akalnya.",
    "en": "In the vision the chief of the Little People also prophesied that Plenty Coups would become chief of his people if he used his wits."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "little-people-of-the-pryor-mountains-c12",
   "source_id": "little-people-of-the-pryor-mountains-s1",
   "quote": "Each year, the Crow made an offering to the Little People at Medicine Rocks",
   "locator": "The Little People of the Pryor Mountains",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Setiap tahun orang Crow memberi persembahan kepada Little People di Medicine Rocks.",
    "en": "Each year the Crow made an offering to the Little People at Medicine Rocks."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "little-people-of-the-pryor-mountains-c13",
   "source_id": "little-people-of-the-pryor-mountains-s2",
   "quote": "The Little People of the Pryor Mountains have been known to the Crow for generations; to many they are spiritual guides and helpers of the Crow, to others they are considered mischievous and are to be avoided.",
   "locator": "Bab 1",
   "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Little People Pryor Mountains telah dikenal orang Crow selama beberapa generasi; bagi banyak orang mereka pemandu spiritual dan penolong, bagi yang lain mereka dianggap jahil dan harus dihindari.",
    "en": "The Little People of the Pryor Mountains have been known to the Crow for generations; to many they are spiritual guides and helpers, to others they are considered mischievous and to be avoided."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "little-people-of-the-pryor-mountains-c14",
   "source_id": "little-people-of-the-pryor-mountains-s2",
   "quote": "Regardless of how they are thought of, the Crow teach respect for these powerful beings; they are to be treated as Elders.",
   "locator": "Bab 1",
   "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Apa pun anggapan orang, Crow mengajarkan hormat kepada makhluk-makhluk berkuasa ini; mereka harus diperlakukan sebagai Elders (tetua).",
    "en": "However they are thought of, the Crow teach respect for these powerful beings, who are to be treated as Elders."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "little-people-of-the-pryor-mountains-c15",
   "source_id": "little-people-of-the-pryor-mountains-s2",
   "quote": "The Little People adopted a Crow boy at a very young age when he was lost in the Pryor Mountains, separated from his Crow family.",
   "locator": "Bab 1",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Dalam salah satu kisah, Little People mengadopsi seorang anak lelaki Crow yang masih sangat kecil ketika ia tersesat di Pryor Mountains, terpisah dari keluarganya.",
    "en": "In one story the Little People adopted a Crow boy at a very young age when he was lost in the Pryor Mountains, separated from his Crow family."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "little-people-of-the-pryor-mountains-c16",
   "source_id": "little-people-of-the-pryor-mountains-s2",
   "quote": "They were told to shoot arrows at the face of the cliff, giving the place the name Arrow Rock, as they passed by for safe passage to their final destination.",
   "locator": "Bab 1",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Orang Crow diminta menembakkan panah ke wajah tebing saat lewat demi jalan aman menuju tujuan, sehingga tempat itu dinamai Arrow Rock.",
    "en": "The Crow were told to shoot arrows at the face of the cliff as they passed for safe passage to their destination, giving the place the name Arrow Rock."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "little-people-of-the-pryor-mountains-c17",
   "source_id": "little-people-of-the-pryor-mountains-s2",
   "quote": "They were also instructed to pile rocks at the base of the cliff as prayer offerings, leave meat for the Little People to eat, and women were told to leave beads and jewelry for the Little Women to find and wear.",
   "locator": "Bab 1",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Mereka juga diperintahkan menumpuk batu di kaki tebing sebagai persembahan doa, meninggalkan daging untuk dimakan Little People, dan perempuan meninggalkan manik dan perhiasan untuk Little Women.",
    "en": "They were also instructed to pile rocks at the base of the cliff as prayer offerings, leave meat for the Little People to eat, and women were told to leave beads and jewelry for the Little Women to wear."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "little-people-of-the-pryor-mountains-c18",
   "source_id": "little-people-of-the-pryor-mountains-s2",
   "quote": "The Little People are described as dwarves that are both human and supernatural beings that can act as spiritual guides for the Crow Tribe.",
   "locator": "Abstrak",
   "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Little People digambarkan sebagai kurcaci yang sekaligus manusia dan makhluk gaib dan dapat menjadi pemandu spiritual bagi Suku Crow.",
    "en": "The Little People are described as dwarves that are both human and supernatural beings and can act as spiritual guides for the Crow Tribe."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "little-people-of-the-pryor-mountains-c19",
   "source_id": "little-people-of-the-pryor-mountains-s2",
   "quote": "They have been described as being between twenty inches and 4 feet in height and look like adults in miniature size, hence the name Little People.",
   "locator": "Bab 1",
   "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Mereka digambarkan setinggi antara dua puluh inci dan 4 kaki dan tampak seperti orang dewasa mini, sehingga dinamai Little People.",
    "en": "They have been described as between twenty inches and 4 feet in height and looking like miniature adults, hence the name Little People."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "little-people-of-the-pryor-mountains-c20",
   "source_id": "little-people-of-the-pryor-mountains-s2",
   "quote": "Based on Crow oral histories Arrow Shot Into Rock is the place where the Crow encountered the Little People for the first time.",
   "locator": "Abstrak",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Berdasarkan sejarah lisan Crow, Arrow Shot Into Rock adalah tempat orang Crow pertama kali bertemu Little People.",
    "en": "Based on Crow oral histories, Arrow Shot Into Rock is the place where the Crow first met the Little People."
   }
  }
 ],

 "traits": [],
 "short_description": {
  "id": "Lurikeen adalah lutin dalam cerita rakyat Irlandia, roh pendamping para tinker; dalam kisah Kildare ia digambarkan sebagai tukang sepatu kecil yang menipu gadis penangkapnya agar lepas.",
  "en": "The Lurikeen is a lutin of Irish folklore and familiar spirit of tinkers; in the Kildare tale he is shown as a tiny cobbler who tricks his captor girl into letting him go.",
  "claim_ids": [
   "lurikeen-c01",
   "lurikeen-c02",
   "lurikeen-c06",
   "lurikeen-c07"
  ]
 },
 "long_description": [
  {
   "id": "Dalam cerita rakyat Irlandia, Lurikeen adalah lutin (peri kecil) sekaligus roh pendamping para tinker, yaitu tukang kaleng keliling, dan dikaitkan dengan kesengsaraan serta kekalahan. Kisah paling jelas tentangnya dalam sumber kedua berasal dari Kildare.",
   "en": "In Irish folklore the Lurikeen is a lutin, a small sprite, and the familiar spirit of tinkers, the itinerant tinsmiths, and is linked with misery and defeats. The clearest tale about him in the second source comes from Kildare.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "lurikeen-c01",
    "lurikeen-c02"
   ]
  },
  {
   "id": "Dalam kisah Kildare, seorang gadis bernama Breedheen menemukan Lurikeen bekerja memperbaiki sepatu kecil di bawah pohon tua. Ia berpakaian topi bersudut tiga berhias renda emas dan celana selutut, dengan kendi bir dan pipa, dan begitu asyik menyanyikan balada Irlandia sampai tidak menyadari gadis itu menangkap tengkuknya.",
   "en": "In the Kildare tale a girl called Breedheen finds the Lurikeen mending a tiny shoe under an old tree. He wears a three-cornered hat with gold lace and knee-breeches, with a beer jug and a pipe, and is so absorbed in singing an Irish ballad that he fails to notice the girl seizing him by the scruff.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "lurikeen-c02",
    "lurikeen-c03",
    "lurikeen-c04"
   ]
  },
  {
   "id": "Gadis itu menuntut uang dan menolak melepaskannya. Di puncak bukit, Lurikeen berteriak seolah Castle Carberry terbakar; ketika gadis itu menoleh ke kastil, ia kehilangan bobotnya di tangannya dan makhluk itu menghilang seakan mimpi.",
   "en": "The girl demands his money and will not let go. At the brow of a hill the Lurikeen screams as if Castle Carberry were on fire; when she looks toward the castle, the weight in her hands is gone and he has vanished as if it had been a dream.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "lurikeen-c05",
    "lurikeen-c06",
    "lurikeen-c07"
   ]
  },
  {
   "id": "Satu kisah Wexford hanya sedikit berbeda: Molly mengawasi Lurikeen sampai ia menunjuk batang booliaun bui tempat harta terkubur, tetapi ketika Molly kembali membawa sekop, seluruh tempat sudah dipenuhi booliaun.",
   "en": "A Wexford tale differs only slightly: Molly watches the Lurikeen until he points out the booliaun bui stalk where treasure lies, but when she returns with spades, the whole place is thick with booliauns.",
   "claim_ids": [

 ],

 "story_mode": {

   "description": {
    "id": "Kisah Wexford hanya sedikit berbeda dari kisah Kildare: Molly mengawasi Lurikeen hingga ia menunjuk batang tempat harta, tetapi seluruh area kemudian dipenuhi booliaun.",

 "stories": [
  {
   "title": {
    "id": "Lurikeen dari Kildare",
    "en": "The Kildare Lurikeen"
   },
   "role": {

   },
   "summary": {
    "id": "Breedheen menangkap Lurikeen yang sedang bekerja dan menuntut uangnya; di puncak bukit ia berteriak bahwa Castle Carberry terbakar, dan lenyap saat gadis itu menoleh.",
    "en": "Breedheen seizes the Lurikeen at work and demands his money; at the brow of the hill he cries that Castle Carberry is afire and vanishes when she turns."

  {

   "author": "D. L. Ashliman (ed.); Patrick Kennedy",

 ],
 "claims": [
  {
   "id": "lurikeen-c01",
   "source_id": "lurikeen-s1",
   "quote": "Dans le folklore irlandais ... le Lurikeen est un lutin et esprit familier des « tinkers » (ferblantiers itinérants), lié à la misère et aux défaites.",
   "locator": "Intro",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Dalam cerita rakyat Irlandia (folklore irlandais), Lurikeen adalah lutin (peri kecil) sekaligus roh pendamping para \"tinker\" (tukang kaleng keliling), dikaitkan dengan kesengsaraan dan kekalahan.",
    "en": "In Irish folklore (folklore irlandais), the Lurikeen is a lutin (sprite) and familiar spirit of tinkers (itinerant tinsmiths), linked to misery and defeats."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "lurikeen-c02",
   "source_id": "lurikeen-s2",
   "quote": "the Lurikeen, working like vengeance at a little old brogue only fit for the foot of a fairy like himself.",
   "locator": "The Kildare Lurikeen",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Dalam kisah Kildare, Lurikeen digambarkan duduk di bawah pohon hawthorn tua, bekerja keras memperbaiki sepatu kecil tua yang hanya pantas untuk kaki peri.",
    "en": "In the Kildare tale the Lurikeen is shown working like vengeance at a little old brogue only fit for the foot of a fairy."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "lurikeen-c03",
   "source_id": "lurikeen-s2",
   "quote": "There he was, boring his holes, and jerking his waxed ends, with his little three-cornered hat with gold lace, his knee-breeches, his jug of beer by his side, and his pipe in his mouth.",
   "locator": "The Kildare Lurikeen",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Ia melubangi sepatu dan menarik benang berlilin, memakai topi kecil bersudut tiga berhias renda emas, celana selutut, dengan kendi bir di sampingnya dan pipa di mulut.",
    "en": "He bores his holes and jerks his waxed ends, wearing a little three-cornered hat with gold lace and knee-breeches, with a jug of beer at his side and a pipe in his mouth."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "lurikeen-c04",
   "source_id": "lurikeen-s2",
   "quote": "He was so busy at his work, and so taken up with an old ballad he was singing in Irish, that he did not mind Breedheen till she had him by the scruff o' the neck",
   "locator": "The Kildare Lurikeen",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Ia begitu sibuk bekerja dan asyik menyanyikan balada lama dalam bahasa Irlandia sampai tidak menyadari Breedheen sudah menangkap tengkuknya.",
    "en": "He was so busy at his work and so taken up singing an old ballad in Irish that he did not notice Breedheen until she had him by the scruff of the neck."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "lurikeen-c05",
   "source_id": "lurikeen-s2",
   "quote": "It's your money I want, and I won't take hand or eye from you till you put me in possession of a fine lob of it.",
   "locator": "The Kildare Lurikeen",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Gadis itu menuntut uang Lurikeen dan menolak melepaskan tangan atau mata darinya sampai ia mendapat segenggam besar.",
    "en": "The girl demands the Lurikeen's money and refuses to take hand or eye from him until she has a fine lob of it."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "lurikeen-c06",
   "source_id": "lurikeen-s2",
   "quote": "he looked up over the ditch, gave a great screetch, and shouted just as if a bugle horn was blew at her ears",
   "locator": "The Kildare Lurikeen",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Di puncak bukit kecil, Lurikeen menengok ke atas parit dan menjerit keras seperti bunyi terompet di telinga gadis itu, berseru bahwa Castle Carberry terbakar.",
    "en": "At the brow of the hill the Lurikeen looked over the ditch, gave a great screech and shouted as if a bugle horn were blown at her ears, crying that Castle Carberry was afire."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "lurikeen-c07",
   "source_id": "lurikeen-s2",
   "quote": "The same moment she missed the weight of the Lurikeen, and when her eyes fell where he was a moment before, there was no more sign of him than if everything that passed was a dream.",
   "locator": "The Kildare Lurikeen",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Saat gadis itu menoleh ke kastil, ia kehilangan bobot Lurikeen di tangannya dan makhluk itu lenyap tanpa jejak, seakan semuanya mimpi.",
    "en": "The moment she turned to the castle she lost the weight of the Lurikeen, and he vanished without a trace, as if it had all been a dream."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "lurikeen-c08",
   "source_id": "lurikeen-s2",
   "quote": "The only instance of a Wexford Lurikeen that we can recall, differs only slightly from this.",
   "locator": "The Kildare Lurikeen",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Satu-satunya contoh Lurikeen dari Wexford yang diingat penulis hanya berbeda sedikit dari kisah Kildare ini.",
    "en": "The only instance of a Wexford Lurikeen the author can recall differs only slightly from this Kildare tale."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "lurikeen-c09",
   "source_id": "lurikeen-s2",
   "quote": "Wexford Molly was as vigilant as Kildare Biddy, and never took eye or hand off him till he pointed out the very stalk of booliaun bui under which the treasure lay.",
   "locator": "The Kildare Lurikeen",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Molly dari Wexford sama waspadanya dengan Biddy dari Kildare dan tidak melepaskan tangan atau mata sampai Lurikeen menunjukkan batang booliaun bui tempat harta terkubur.",
    "en": "Wexford Molly was as vigilant as Kildare Biddy and did not take eye or hand off him until he pointed out the very stalk of booliaun bui under which the treasure lay."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "lurikeen-c10",
   "source_id": "lurikeen-s2",
   "quote": "when Molly returned in half an hour, attended by father and brothers with spades and picks, all round the spot, to a considerable distance, was as thick with booliauns as a plantation with young trees.",
   "locator": "The Kildare Lurikeen",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Ketika Molly kembali setengah jam kemudian bersama ayah dan saudara-saudaranya membawa sekop dan beliung, seluruh area itu sudah penuh booliaun seperti kebun pohon muda.",
    "en": "When Molly returned half an hour later with her father and brothers carrying spades and picks, the whole spot was as thick with booliauns as a plantation of young trees."
   }
  }
 ],

 "short_description": {
  "id": "Maelor Gawr adalah raja Kelt awal sekaligus raksasa dalam mitologi Wales yang tinggal di Castell Maelor dan kisahnya dicatat bersama tiga putranya dalam teks akhir abad ke-16.",
  "en": "Maelor Gawr, a giant and early Celtic king in Welsh mythology, lived at Castell Maelor; his tale, with his three sons, is preserved in a Welsh text of the late 1500s.",

 "long_description": [
  {
   "id": "Maelor Gawr dikenal sebagai raja Kelt awal dan raksasa dalam mitologi Wales. Kisahnya bersama tiga putranya, Cornippyn, Crygyn, dan Bwba, tercatat dalam teks Wales akhir abad ke-16; teks itu mengejanya Maylor Gawr dan menempatkannya di Aberteifi sebelum kedatangan Brutus.",
   "en": "Maelor Gawr is known as an early Celtic king and giant of Welsh mythology. His tale with his three sons, Cornippyn, Crygyn and Bwba, is recorded in a late sixteenth-century Welsh text; that text spells him Maylor Gawr and places him in Aberteifi before the coming of Brutus.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "maelor-gawr-c01",
    "maelor-gawr-c02",
    "maelor-gawr-c03",
    "maelor-gawr-c07",
    "maelor-gawr-c08"
   ]
  },
  {
   "id": "Menurut teks itu, Maylor ditawan di Cyfeilog, sekitar dua belas mil dari kastilnya, dan sebelum dihukum mati ia diizinkan meniup tanduknya tiga kali. Tiupan pertama merontokkan rambut dan jenggotnya, tiupan kedua merontokkan semua kukunya, dan pada tiupan ketiga tanduk itu pecah berkeping-keping.",
   "en": "According to the text, Maylor was taken prisoner at Cyfeilog, about twelve miles from his castle, and before being put to death was allowed to blow his horn three times. The first blast made his hair and beard fall out, the second his nails, and at the third the horn shattered into small pieces.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "maelor-gawr-c09",
    "maelor-gawr-c10",
    "maelor-gawr-c11",
    "maelor-gawr-c04"
   ]
  },
  {
   "id": "Cornippin, putranya, mendengar bunyi tanduk itu dan memacu kudanya demikian cepat hingga kepala anjing pemburunya terlepas; kudanya melompati Sungai Ystwyth dalam satu lompatan. Setelah bertarung, ia terbunuh. Dua putra lain, Crygyn dan Bwba, membunuh siapa pun yang mendekati benteng mereka.",
   "en": "His son Cornippin heard the horn and rode so fast that his hound's head was torn off; his horse cleared the Ystwyth River in a single bound. After fighting he was killed. The other two sons, Crygyn and Bwba, killed anyone who came near their strongholds.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "maelor-gawr-c12",
    "maelor-gawr-c13",
    "maelor-gawr-c14",
    "maelor-gawr-c05"
   ]
  },
  {

   "en": "Present-day stories in Penparcau still tell of the headless dog of King Maelor's son.",

 "claims": [
  {
   "id": "maelor-gawr-c01",
   "source_id": "maelor-gawr-s1",
   "quote": "Maelor Gawr is an early Celtic king and giant of Welsh mythology",
   "locator": "Intro",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Maelor Gawr adalah raja Kelt awal sekaligus raksasa dalam mitologi Wales.",
    "en": "Maelor Gawr is an early Celtic king and giant of Welsh mythology."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "maelor-gawr-c02",
   "source_id": "maelor-gawr-s1",
   "quote": "The tale of Maelor and his three sons, Cornippyn, Crygyn and Bwba, is recorded in the late sixteenth century Welsh text",
   "locator": "Intro",
   "context": "historical-record",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Kisah Maelor dan ketiga putranya, Cornippyn, Crygyn, dan Bwba, tercatat dalam teks Wales akhir abad ke-16.",
    "en": "The tale of Maelor and his three sons, Cornippyn, Crygyn and Bwba, is recorded in a late sixteenth-century Welsh text."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "maelor-gawr-c03",
   "source_id": "maelor-gawr-s1",
   "quote": "with the antiquarian spelling Maylor Gawr",
   "locator": "Intro",
   "context": "historical-record",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Teks lama menulis namanya dengan ejaan antikuaris Maylor Gawr.",
    "en": "The older text uses the antiquarian spelling Maylor Gawr."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "maelor-gawr-c04",
   "source_id": "maelor-gawr-s1",
   "quote": "On the third blast of his horn the intensity of the force of the sound caused the horn to be broken into small pieces.",
   "locator": "Intro",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Pada tiupan ketiga, kerasnya bunyi membuat tanduk itu pecah berkeping-keping.",
    "en": "On the third blast the intensity of the sound caused the horn to break into small pieces."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "maelor-gawr-c05",
   "source_id": "maelor-gawr-s1",
   "quote": "The other sons, Crygyn and Bwba, lived in Llanilar and Llanbadarn Fawr respectively, and murdered any man who approached their stronghold.",
   "locator": "Intro",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Putra lainnya, Crygyn dan Bwba, tinggal masing-masing di Llanilar dan Llanbadarn Fawr dan membunuh siapa pun yang mendekati benteng mereka.",
    "en": "The other sons, Crygyn and Bwba, lived in Llanilar and Llanbadarn Fawr respectively and murdered any man who approached their stronghold."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "maelor-gawr-c06",
   "source_id": "maelor-gawr-s1",
   "quote": "Contemporary stories still relate to one of the more unusual residents of Penparcau village, the headless dog of King Maelor",
   "locator": "Myth, folklore and legend",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Cerita masa kini masih mengisahkan salah satu penghuni Penparcau yang tak biasa, anjing tanpa kepala milik putra Raja Maelor.",
    "en": "Contemporary stories still tell of one of the more unusual residents of Penparcau village, the headless dog of the son of King Maelor."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "maelor-gawr-c07",
   "source_id": "maelor-gawr-s2",
   "quote": "And in the country of Aberteifi, before the coming of Brutus to this island, there formerly lived Maylor Gawr, and the place where he lived is still called Castell Maylor",
   "locator": "Teks Rhys",
   "context": "historical-record",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Menurut Rhys, di negeri Aberteifi sebelum kedatangan Brutus ke pulau ini pernah tinggal Maylor Gawr, dan tempat tinggalnya masih disebut Castell Maylor.",
    "en": "According to Rhys, in the country of Aberteifi before Brutus came to this island there lived Maylor Gawr, and his dwelling is still called Castell Maylor."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "maelor-gawr-c08",
   "source_id": "maelor-gawr-s2",
   "quote": "To this Maylor Gawr were three sons, namely, Cornippin Gawr, and Crygyn Gawr, and Bwba Gawr.",
   "locator": "Teks Rhys",
   "context": "historical-record",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Maylor Gawr memiliki tiga putra: Cornippin Gawr, Crygyn Gawr, dan Bwba Gawr.",
    "en": "Maylor Gawr had three sons: Cornippin Gawr, Crygyn Gawr and Bwba Gawr."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "maelor-gawr-c09",
   "source_id": "maelor-gawr-s2",
   "quote": "And it came to pass that Maylor Gawr was taken prisoner in a place called Cyfeilog, about twelve miles from his own castle",
   "locator": "Teks Rhys",
   "context": "historical-record",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Maylor Gawr ditawan di tempat bernama Cyfeilog, sekitar dua belas mil dari kastilnya sendiri.",
    "en": "Maylor Gawr was taken prisoner at a place called Cyfeilog, about twelve miles from his own castle."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "maelor-gawr-c10",
   "source_id": "maelor-gawr-s2",
   "quote": "he begged of his enemies to permit him to blow his horn three times before suffering death",
   "locator": "Teks Rhys",
   "context": "historical-record",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Saat hendak dihukum mati, ia memohon kepada musuhnya agar boleh meniup tanduknya tiga kali.",
    "en": "When about to be put to death he begged his enemies to let him blow his horn three times."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "maelor-gawr-c11",
   "source_id": "maelor-gawr-s2",
   "quote": "And on the second blast of his horn, so great was the strength and force of the sounding that all his finger and toe-nails fell off completely.",
   "locator": "Teks Rhys",
   "context": "historical-record",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Pada tiupan kedua, kekuatan bunyinya begitu besar hingga semua kuku jari tangan dan kakinya rontok.",
    "en": "On the second blast, so great was the force of the sound that all his finger and toe-nails fell off."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "maelor-gawr-c12",
   "source_id": "maelor-gawr-s2",
   "quote": "in riding with such haste and swiftness, he tore the head of his hound off its body",
   "locator": "Teks Rhys",
   "context": "historical-record",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Cornippin menunggang dengan begitu tergesa dan cepat hingga kepala anjing pemburunya terlepas dari tubuhnya.",
    "en": "Cornippin rode with such haste and swiftness that he tore the head of his hound from its body."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "maelor-gawr-c13",
   "source_id": "maelor-gawr-s2",
   "quote": "he spurred his steed until the horse leapt at one bound over the Ystwyth River",
   "locator": "Teks Rhys",
   "context": "historical-record",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Ia memacu kudanya sampai kuda itu melompat dalam satu lompatan melewati Sungai Ystwyth.",
    "en": "He spurred his steed until the horse leapt in a single bound over the Ystwyth River."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "maelor-gawr-c14",
   "source_id": "maelor-gawr-s2",
   "quote": "Cornippin came up to his father, where after fighting he also was killed.",
   "locator": "Teks Rhys",
   "context": "historical-record",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Cornippin sampai ke ayahnya, dan setelah bertarung ia pun terbunuh.",
    "en": "Cornippin reached his father and, after fighting, was also killed."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "maelor-gawr-c15",
   "source_id": "maelor-gawr-s2",
   "quote": "These giants lived in Wales before Brutus came to this island, and their custom while they lived was to kill whatever men should come to lodge within their strongholds",
   "locator": "Teks Rhys",
   "context": "historical-record",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Raksasa-raksasa ini hidup di Wales sebelum Brutus datang ke pulau ini, dan kebiasaan mereka ialah membunuh siapa saja yang datang menginap di benteng mereka.",
    "en": "These giants lived in Wales before Brutus came to this island, and their custom was to kill whatever men came to lodge within their strongholds."
   }
  }
 ],

 ]

  "id": "Petticoat Loose adalah hantu perempuan dalam cerita rakyat Munster yang dipercaya menyerang pelancong pada malam hari, dengan beberapa versi tentang sebab ia menjadi hantu.",
  "en": "Petticoat Loose is a woman's ghost in the folklore of Munster believed to attack travellers at night, with several accounts of why she became a ghost.",

 "long_description": [
  {
   "id": "Petticoat Loose adalah roh atau hantu seorang perempuan dalam cerita rakyat Munster. Ia dipercaya menyerang pelancong pada malam hari, menakut-nakuti dan menyiksa mereka. Béal Locha di County Waterford disebut sebagai tempat asalnya, meski ada yang mengatakan dari Coilleagán, dan cerita rakyat menyebut ia terlihat di seluruh Munster.",
   "en": "Petticoat Loose is the spirit or ghost of a woman in Munster folklore. She is believed to attack travellers at night, frightening and tormenting them. Béal Locha in County Waterford is named as her home place, though some say Coilleagán, and folklore says she can be seen across Munster.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "petticoat-loose-c01",
    "petticoat-loose-c02",
    "petticoat-loose-c03"
   ]
  },
  {
   "id": "Menurut cerita rakyat namanya Cáit atau Máire Ní Annagáin dan ia menikah dengan seorang Ó Scanláin. Berbagai sebab dikemukakan atas statusnya sebagai hantu, antara lain ia ganas dan memukul ibunya sendiri, atau ia membunuh bayi yang belum dibaptis. Satu versi mengaitkan julukannya dengan malam hiburan ketika roknya robek saat ia menari liar.",
   "en": "According to folklore her name was Cáit or Máire Ní Annagáin and she was married to an Ó Scanláin. Various reasons are given for her being a ghost, among them that she was wild and struck her own mother, or that she killed an unbaptised child. One version links her nickname to an evening's entertainment when her skirt tore as she danced wildly.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "petticoat-loose-c04",
    "petticoat-loose-c05",
    "petticoat-loose-c06"
   ]
  },
  {
   "id": "Dalam catatan dari arsip Sekolah dúchas.ie, ia perempuan malang yang dibunuh di jalan menuju Co. Kilkenny lalu menghantui jalan itu dan tidak membiarkan kereta lewat. Ia mengaku kepada pastor bahwa ia membunuh anaknya sendiri tanpa baptis; pastor berkata akan mengusirnya ke Laut Merah, dan ia membalas tidak akan membiarkan kapal lewat tanpa tenggelam.",
   "en": "In a record from the dúchas.ie Schools' Collection she is an unfortunate woman killed on the road to Co. Kilkenny who then haunted it and would let no car pass. She told a priest she killed her own child without baptism; the priest said he would banish her to the Red Sea, and she replied she would let no ship pass without drowning it.",

 "claims": [
  {
   "id": "petticoat-loose-c01",
   "source_id": "petticoat-loose-s1",
   "quote": "Is éard atá i gceist le Petticoat Loose ná sprid nó taibhse mná agus í ina hábhar scéalta i mbéaloideas na Mumhan.",
   "locator": "Intro",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Petticoat Loose adalah roh atau hantu (taibhse) seorang perempuan yang menjadi tokoh cerita dalam cerita rakyat (béaloideas) Munster (Mumhan).",
    "en": "Petticoat Loose is the spirit or ghost (taibhse) of a woman who features in the folklore (béaloideas) of Munster (Mumhan)."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "petticoat-loose-c02",
   "source_id": "petticoat-loose-s1",
   "quote": "Mar a chreidtear, bíonn Petticoat Loose ag ionsaí taistealaithe istoíche, á n-imeaglú agus á gcrá.",
   "locator": "Intro",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Dipercaya Petticoat Loose menyerang para pelancong (taistealaithe) pada malam hari, menakut-nakuti dan menyiksa mereka.",
    "en": "Petticoat Loose is believed to attack travellers (taistealaithe) at night, frightening and tormenting them."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "petticoat-loose-c03",
   "source_id": "petticoat-loose-s1",
   "quote": "Is é Béal Locha, Contae Phort Láirge, is áit dúchais do Phetticoat (ach deirtear freisin gurb as Coilleagán sa chontae chéanna di), ach deir an béaloideas go mbíonn sí le feiceáil fud fad na Mumhan.",
   "locator": "Intro",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Béal Locha di Contae Phort Láirge (County Waterford) disebut tempat asalnya, meski ada yang mengatakan dari Coilleagán di county yang sama, dan cerita rakyat menyebut ia terlihat di seluruh Munster.",
    "en": "Béal Locha in County Waterford (Contae Phort Láirge) is named as her home place, though some say she was from Coilleagán in the same county, and folklore says she can be seen throughout Munster."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "petticoat-loose-c04",
   "source_id": "petticoat-loose-s1",
   "quote": "De réir an bhéaloidis is é an t-ainm agus an sloinne a bhí ar Phetticoat ná Cáit (nó Máire) Ní Annagáin, agus bhí sí pósta ar fhear dar shloinne Ó Scanláin.",
   "locator": "Cúlra",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Menurut cerita rakyat, nama Petticoat adalah Cáit (atau Máire) Ní Annagáin, dan ia menikah dengan seorang pria bermarga Ó Scanláin.",
    "en": "According to folklore Petticoat's name was Cáit (or Máire) Ní Annagáin, and she was married to a man surnamed Ó Scanláin."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "petticoat-loose-c05",
   "source_id": "petticoat-loose-s1",
   "quote": "Bhí sí fiain feargach, agus bhuaileadh sí a máthair féin. Mharaigh sí leanbh gan bhaiste.",
   "locator": "Cúlra",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Sebab ia menjadi hantu antara lain: ia ganas dan pemarah serta memukul ibunya sendiri, dan ia membunuh seorang bayi yang belum dibaptis (leanbh gan bhaiste).",
    "en": "Among the reasons given for her becoming a ghost: she was wild and angry and struck her own mother, and she killed an unbaptised child (leanbh gan bhaiste)."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "petticoat-loose-c06",
   "source_id": "petticoat-loose-s1",
   "quote": "nuair a ghreamaigh a sciorta de thairne sa bhalla. Thosaigh sí ag damhsa go fiain, agus ansin réabadh an sciorta di, ionas nár fágadh ach an peireacót uirthi.",
   "locator": "Cúlra",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Menurut satu versi, namanya berasal dari malam hiburan ketika roknya (sciorta) tersangkut paku di dinding; ia menari liar sampai rok itu robek dan hanya petticoat yang tersisa.",
    "en": "In one version her name comes from a night of entertainment when her skirt (sciorta) caught on a nail in the wall; she danced wildly until the skirt tore and only the petticoat was left."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "petticoat-loose-c07",
   "source_id": "petticoat-loose-s1",
   "quote": "Scríobh an drámadóir Éireannach M. J. Molloy dráma faoin teideal Petticoat Loose sa bhliain 1979.",
   "locator": "Sa Litríocht",
   "context": "modern-popular-culture",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Dramawan Irlandia M. J. Molloy menulis sebuah drama berjudul Petticoat Loose pada 1979.",
    "en": "The Irish dramatist M. J. Molloy wrote a play titled Petticoat Loose in 1979."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "petticoat-loose-c08",
   "source_id": "petticoat-loose-s2",
   "quote": "Petticoat Loose was an unfortunate woman. She was killed on the road to Co. Kilkenny, and then she haunted it. She would not let a car pass.",
   "locator": "Halaman 175",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Dalam arsip Sekolah dúchas.ie, Petticoat Loose adalah perempuan malang yang dibunuh di jalan menuju Co. Kilkenny lalu menghantui jalan itu dan tidak membiarkan kereta lewat.",
    "en": "In the dúchas.ie Schools' Collection, Petticoat Loose was an unfortunate woman killed on the road to Co. Kilkenny who then haunted it and would not let a car pass."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "petticoat-loose-c09",
   "source_id": "petticoat-loose-s2",
   "quote": "Then she told him that she had killed her own child without baptism, and the priest said that that was what damned her.",
   "locator": "Halaman 175",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Perempuan itu mengaku telah membunuh anaknya sendiri tanpa baptis, dan pastor berkata itulah yang membuatnya terkutuk.",
    "en": "She told the priest she had killed her own child without baptism, and the priest said that was what damned her."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "petticoat-loose-c10",
   "source_id": "petticoat-loose-s2",
   "quote": "The priest said that he would banish her to the Red Sea. She said if he would she would not leave a ship or a steamer pass but she would drown.",
   "locator": "Halaman 175",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Pastor berkata akan mengusirnya ke Laut Merah; ia menjawab bila itu dilakukan ia tidak akan membiarkan kapal atau kapal uap lewat tanpa tenggelam.",
    "en": "The priest said he would banish her to the Red Sea; she replied that if he did she would let no ship or steamer pass without drowning it."
   }
  }
 ],
 "conflicts": [
  {
   "topic": {
    "id": "Sebab ia menjadi hantu",
    "en": "Why she became a ghost"
   },
   "positions": [

     "summary": {
      "id": "Artikel pertama menyebut beberapa sebab, antara lain memukul ibunya dan membunuh bayi yang belum dibaptis.",

 "traits": [],
 "short_description": {
  "id": "Portunes adalah makhluk kecil bermuka tua dalam cerita rakyat Inggris yang menurut Gervase dari Tilbury masuk ke rumah petani pada malam hari, membantu pekerjaan berat, dan sesekali mengerjai penunggang kuda.",
  "en": "The Portunes are small, old-faced creatures of English folklore who, according to Gervase of Tilbury, enter farmers' houses at night, help with heavy work, and sometimes prank lone riders.",
  "claim_ids": [
   "portunes-c01",
   "portunes-c02",
   "portunes-c09",
   "portunes-c11"
  ]
 },
 "long_description": [
  {
   "id": "Portunes, atau Neptunes, adalah makhluk cerita rakyat Inggris yang oleh Gervase dari Tilbury hanya dijelaskan dalam karyanya Otia Imperialia dari abad pertengahan. Gervase menyebutnya iblis tetapi ragu apakah mereka lebih tepat disebut sosok yang asal-usulnya tak diketahui; orang Prancis menyebutnya Neptunes dan orang Inggris Portunes.",
   "en": "The Portunes, also called Neptunes, are English folklore creatures that Gervase of Tilbury describes solely in his medieval Latin Otia Imperialia. Gervase calls them demons but doubts whether they are better described as figures of unknown origin; the French call them Neptunes and the English Portunes.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "portunes-c01",
    "portunes-c07"
   ]
  },
  {
   "id": "Mereka memasuki rumah petani pada malam hari, menghangatkan diri di dekat api, dan memanggang katak kecil yang mereka bawa. Wajah mereka seperti lelaki tua dengan pipi berkerut, dan tubuh mereka sangat kecil. Mereka membantu pekerjaan berat dan menyelesaikannya lebih cepat daripada manusia, dan sifat mereka melayani, bukan mencelakai.",
   "en": "By night they come into farmers' houses, warm up at the hearth, and grill little frogs that they bring along. Their faces are like old men's with wrinkled cheeks, and their bodies are very small. They help with heavy work and finish it faster than any man, and their nature is to serve, not to harm.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "portunes-c02",
    "portunes-c08",
    "portunes-c09",
    "portunes-c10"
   ]
  },
  {
   "id": "Satu kebiasaan mengganggu mereka: bergabung tanpa terlihat dengan penunggang kuda yang sendirian pada malam hari, mengambil kendali, menuntun kuda ke lumpur, lalu pergi dengan tawa keras.",
   "en": "They have one annoying habit: invisibly joining a lone rider at night, taking the reins, leading the horse into a slough, and going off with a loud laugh.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "portunes-c11"
   ]
  },
  {
   "id": "Para ahli berbeda pendapat. K. M. Briggs menggolongkan mereka sebagai peri, C. C. Oman menilai keterangan ukurannya keliru, dan Banks serta Binns membantahnya. Young menilai kaitan langsung antara nama Inggris itu dan dewa Romawi Portunus tidak mungkin.",
   "en": "Scholars differ. K. M. Briggs classes them as fairies, C. C. Oman thinks the description of their size is corrupted, and Banks and Binns dispute that. Young judges a direct link between the English name and the Roman god Portunus unlikely.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "portunes-c03",
    "portunes-c04",

   ]
  }

   "source_id": "portunes-s1",
   "quote": "Portunes, or Neptunes, are creatures of English folklore described only in the medieval Latin work Otia Imperialia by Gervase of Tilbury.",
   "locator": "Intro",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Portunes, atau Neptunes, adalah makhluk cerita rakyat Inggris yang hanya dijelaskan dalam karya Latin abad pertengahan Otia Imperialia oleh Gervase dari Tilbury.",
    "en": "Portunes, or Neptunes, are creatures of English folklore described only in the medieval Latin work Otia Imperialia by Gervase of Tilbury."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "portunes-c02",
   "source_id": "portunes-s1",
   "quote": "Portunes enter peasant houses at night, warm themselves by the fire, and roast small frogs which they carry in their pockets.",
   "locator": "Intro",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Dalam catatan Gervase, Portunes memasuki rumah petani pada malam hari, menghangatkan diri di dekat api, dan memanggang katak kecil yang mereka bawa di saku.",
    "en": "In Gervase's account Portunes enter peasant houses at night, warm themselves by the fire, and roast small frogs which they carry in their pockets."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "portunes-c03",
   "source_id": "portunes-s1",
   "quote": "K. M. Briggs classes them as a type of fairy, arguing that Gervase's account was the first description of small English fairies.",
   "locator": "Intro",
   "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
   "statement": {
    "id": "K. M. Briggs menggolongkan mereka sebagai sejenis peri dan berpendapat catatan Gervase adalah gambaran pertama tentang peri-peri kecil Inggris.",
    "en": "K. M. Briggs classes them as a type of fairy, arguing that Gervase's account was the first description of small English fairies."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "portunes-c04",
   "source_id": "portunes-s1",
   "quote": "C. C. Oman argues that the description of the size of the Portunes is corrupted because the size of the creatures is incompatible with the housework they do",
   "locator": "Intro",
   "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
   "statement": {
    "id": "C. C. Oman berpendapat bahwa keterangan ukuran Portunes keliru karena ukuran itu tidak sesuai dengan pekerjaan rumah yang mereka lakukan.",
    "en": "C. C. Oman argues that the description of the size of the Portunes is corrupted because their size is incompatible with the housework they do."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "portunes-c05",
   "source_id": "portunes-s1",
   "quote": "S. E. Banks and J. W. Binns dispute this, arguing that the manuscripts are in agreement and Gervase clearly intended to describe them as very small.",
   "locator": "Intro",
   "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
   "statement": {
    "id": "S. E. Banks dan J. W. Binns membantahnya, dengan alasan naskah-naskah sepakat dan Gervase jelas bermaksud menggambarkan mereka sangat kecil.",
    "en": "S. E. Banks and J. W. Binns dispute this, arguing that the manuscripts agree and Gervase clearly meant to describe them as very small."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "portunes-c06",
   "source_id": "portunes-s1",
   "quote": "Young also considers a link between the English name and the Roman god Portunus, but argues that a direct relationship is unlikely because there is no evidence for a cult of Portunus in Roman Britain",
   "locator": "Naming",
   "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Young mempertimbangkan kaitan antara nama Inggris itu dan dewa Romawi Portunus, tetapi menilai hubungan langsung tidak mungkin karena tidak ada bukti pemujaan Portunus di Britania Romawi.",
    "en": "Young considers a link between the English name and the Roman god Portunus but argues a direct relationship is unlikely because there is no evidence for a cult of Portunus in Roman Britain."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "portunes-c07",
   "source_id": "portunes-s2",
   "quote": "They have in England certain demons, though I know not whether I should call them demons or figures of a secret and unknown generation, which the French call Neptunes, the English Portunes.",
   "locator": "Bab The Portunes",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Gervase (dikutip Keightley) menulis bahwa di Inggris ada semacam iblis (demons), meski ia ragu apakah harus menyebutnya iblis atau sosok yang asal-usulnya rahasia dan tak diketahui; orang Prancis menyebutnya Neptunes, orang Inggris Portunes.",
    "en": "Gervase (as quoted by Keightley) writes that in England there are certain demons, though he doubts whether to call them demons or figures of a secret and unknown generation, which the French call Neptunes and the English Portunes."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "portunes-c08",
   "source_id": "portunes-s2",
   "quote": "It is their nature to embrace the simple life of comfortable farmers",
   "locator": "Bab The Portunes",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Sudah menjadi sifat mereka untuk menjalani hidup sederhana para petani yang berkecukupan.",
    "en": "It is their nature to embrace the simple life of comfortable farmers."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "portunes-c09",
   "source_id": "portunes-s2",
   "quote": "They have the countenance of old men, with wrinkled cheeks, and they are of a very small stature, not being quite half-an-inch high.",
   "locator": "Bab The Portunes",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Mereka berwajah lelaki tua dengan pipi berkerut dan bertubuh sangat kecil, tingginya tidak sampai setengah inci.",
    "en": "They have the countenance of old men with wrinkled cheeks and are of very small stature, not quite half-an-inch high."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "portunes-c10",
   "source_id": "portunes-s2",
   "quote": "if anything is to be carried into the house, or any laborious work to be done, they lend a hand, and finish it sooner than any man could. It is their nature to have the power to serve, but not to injure.",
   "locator": "Bab The Portunes",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Bila ada barang yang harus dibawa ke rumah atau pekerjaan berat yang harus dilakukan, mereka membantu dan menyelesaikannya lebih cepat daripada manusia mana pun; sifat mereka berkuasa melayani tetapi tidak mencelakai.",
    "en": "If anything is to be carried into the house or any laborious work done, they lend a hand and finish it sooner than any man could; their nature is to have the power to serve but not to injure."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "portunes-c11",
   "source_id": "portunes-s2",
   "quote": "the Portune sometimes invisibly joins the horseman; and when he has accompanied him a good while, he at last takes the reins, and leads the horse into a neighbouring slough; and when he is fixed and floundering in it, the Portune goes off with a loud laugh",
   "locator": "Bab The Portunes",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Portune kadang bergabung tanpa terlihat dengan penunggang kuda yang sendirian, akhirnya mengambil kendali dan menuntun kuda ke lumpur, lalu pergi dengan tawa keras ketika kuda itu terperosok.",
    "en": "The Portune sometimes invisibly joins a lone horseman, at last takes the reins and leads the horse into a slough, then goes off with a loud laugh once the horse is floundering in it."
   }
  }
 ],

 "short_description": {
  "id": "Pyewacket adalah roh pendamping (familiar) yang disebut dalam pengakuan seorang perempuan yang dituduh Matthew Hopkins sebagai penyihir di Manningtree, Essex, pada Maret 1644.",
  "en": "Pyewacket is a familiar spirit named in the confession of a woman accused of witchcraft by Matthew Hopkins at Manningtree, Essex, in March 1644.",
  "claim_ids": [
   "pyewacket-familiar-spirit-c01",

  ]
 },
 "long_description": [
  {
   "id": "Pyewacket dikatakan salah satu roh pendamping seorang penyihir terpidana yang dituduh Matthew Hopkins pada Maret 1644 di kota Manningtree, Essex, Inggris. Hopkins mengaku memata-matai pertemuan para penyihir dekat rumahnya dan mendengar mereka menyebut nama seorang perempuan setempat.",
   "en": "One of the familiar spirits attributed to a convicted witch, Pyewacket belongs to the case brought by Matthew Hopkins in March 1644 at Manningtree, Essex, England. Hopkins said he secretly watched the witches meet close to his home and overheard a local woman being named.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "pyewacket-familiar-spirit-c01",
    "pyewacket-familiar-spirit-c02",
    "pyewacket-familiar-spirit-c07"
   ]
  },
  {
   "id": "Perempuan itu ditangkap dan dicegah tidur empat malam, lalu mengaku dan menyebut nama roh pendampingnya beserta bentuk yang akan mereka ambil. Menurut Hopkins, ia memanggil mereka pada malam keempat dengan sepuluh orang hadir, dan menyebut nama imp lain, antara lain Elemanzer, Pyewacket, dan Peckin the Crown.",
   "en": "The woman was arrested and kept from sleep for four nights, then confessed and named her familiars and the forms they would take. According to Hopkins she called them on the fourth night with ten people present, and named other imps, including Elemanzer, Pyewacket and Peckin the Crown.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "pyewacket-familiar-spirit-c03",
    "pyewacket-familiar-spirit-c08",
    "pyewacket-familiar-spirit-c09"
   ]
  },
  {
   "id": "Nama Pyewacket kemudian dipakai dalam budaya populer: kucing Siam milik penyihir dalam film Bell, Book and Candle (1958), serta judul sebuah film horor 2017.",
   "en": "The name Pyewacket was later reused in popular culture: as the witch's Siamese cat in the film Bell, Book and Candle (1958) and as the title of a 2017 horror film.",
   "claim_ids": [

   "id": "Dalam mitologi Skotlandia, Searrach Uisge disebut sebagai 'monster' yang tinggal di danau Loch Suainbhal. Sama seperti Nessie dan Morag, keberadaannya tidak pernah dibuktikan.",
   "en": "In Scottish mythology the Searrach Uisge is named as a 'monster' living in Loch Suainbhal. One folklore site suggests it may be a water horse and says it was said to have inhabited the loch for over a century. As with Nessie and Morag, its existence has never been proven.",

   "id": "Makhluk ini digambarkan berbentuk seperti perahu yang terbalik ketika terlihat berenang, dengan panjang sekitar sepuluh hingga tiga belas meter. Gambaran lain menyebutnya sepanjang empat puluh kaki dan mirip belut. Kesaksian pertama tentang makhluk ini berasal dari abad ke-19.",
   "en": "It is described as shaped like a capsized boat when seen swimming, some ten to thirteen metres long. Another description makes it forty feet long and eel-like. The first sightings of the creature date from the 19th century.",

   "author": "Ian Topham",
   "publisher": "Mysterious Britain & Ireland",
   "published": "2009-06-17",
   "language": "en",
   "type": "other",
   "accessed": "2026-10-03"
  }
 ],
 "claims": [
  {
   "id": "searrach-uisge-c01",
   "source_id": "searrach-uisge-s1",
   "quote": "Searrach Uisge is de naam van een 'monster' uit de Schotse mythologie dat zou leven in het Schotse Loch Suainbhal.",
   "locator": "Intro",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Searrach Uisge adalah nama sebuah 'monster' (monster) dari mitologi Skotlandia (Schotse mythologie) yang dikatakan hidup di Loch Suainbhal di Skotlandia.",
    "en": "Searrach Uisge is the name of a 'monster' from Scottish mythology (Schotse mythologie) said to live (zou leven) in the Scottish Loch Suainbhal."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "searrach-uisge-c02",
   "source_id": "searrach-uisge-s1",
   "quote": "Het bestaan van dit wezen is evenals dat van landgenoten Nessie en Morag nooit aangetoond.",
   "locator": "Intro",
   "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Keberadaan makhluk ini (wezen), seperti halnya Nessie dan Morag, tidak pernah dibuktikan (nooit aangetoond).",
    "en": "The existence (bestaan) of this creature (wezen), like that of its compatriots Nessie and Morag, has never been proven (nooit aangetoond)."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "searrach-uisge-c03",
   "source_id": "searrach-uisge-s1",
   "quote": "Searrach Uisge zou de vorm hebben van een gekapseisde boot en tien tot dertien meter lang zijn.",
   "locator": "Intro",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Searrach Uisge dikatakan berbentuk perahu terbalik (gekapseisde boot) dan panjangnya sepuluh hingga tiga belas meter (tien tot dertien meter).",
    "en": "The Searrach Uisge is said to have the shape (vorm) of a capsized boat (gekapseisde boot) and be ten to thirteen metres (tien tot dertien meter) long."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "searrach-uisge-c04",
   "source_id": "searrach-uisge-s1",
   "quote": "De eerste getuigenissen die verklaren het wezen gezien te hebben, dateren uit de vroege tweede helft van de 19e eeuw.",
   "locator": "Intro",
   "context": "historical-record",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Kesaksian pertama (eerste getuigenissen) tentang makhluk ini berasal dari awal paruh kedua abad ke-19 (19e eeuw).",
    "en": "The first testimonies (eerste getuigenissen) of having seen the creature date from the early second half of the 19th century (19e eeuw)."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "searrach-uisge-c05",
   "source_id": "searrach-uisge-s1",
   "quote": "Volgens de overlevering offerde vroeger de lokale bevolking eens per jaar lammeren aan Searrach Uisge.",
   "locator": "Intro",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Menurut tradisi (overlevering), penduduk setempat dahulu mempersembahkan anak domba (lammeren) kepada Searrach Uisge setahun sekali (eens per jaar).",
    "en": "According to tradition (overlevering), the local population once offered lambs (lammeren) to the Searrach Uisge once a year (eens per jaar)."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "searrach-uisge-c06",
   "source_id": "searrach-uisge-s2",
   "quote": "The creature, possibly a water horse of Loch Suainbhal is referred to as the Searrach Uisge.",
   "locator": "Main text",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Makhluk yang disebut Searrach Uisge mungkin adalah kuda air dari Loch Suainbhal.",
    "en": "The creature referred to as the Searrach Uisge is possibly a water horse of Loch Suainbhal."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "searrach-uisge-c07",
   "source_id": "searrach-uisge-s2",
   "quote": "The creature was said to inhabit the loch for over a century and in the nineteenth century it was described as being like an capsized boat when it was seen swimming around.",
   "locator": "Main text",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Makhluk ini dikatakan menghuni danau itu lebih dari satu abad, dan pada abad ke-19 ia digambarkan seperti perahu terbalik saat terlihat berenang.",
    "en": "The creature was said to have inhabited the loch for over a century, and in the nineteenth century it was described as like a capsized boat when seen swimming around."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "searrach-uisge-c08",
   "source_id": "searrach-uisge-s2",
   "quote": "Another description has it being forty foot long and eel like.",
   "locator": "Main text",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Gambaran lain menyebutnya sepanjang empat puluh kaki dan mirip belut.",
    "en": "Another description has it forty feet long and eel-like."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "searrach-uisge-c09",
   "source_id": "searrach-uisge-s2",
   "quote": "It is said that lambs would be offered to the creature by the locals as an annual sacrifice to appease the beast.",
   "locator": "Main text",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Konon penduduk setempat mempersembahkan anak domba kepada makhluk ini sebagai korban tahunan untuk menenangkannya.",
    "en": "It is said that locals offered lambs to the creature as an annual sacrifice to appease the beast."
   }
  }
 ],
 "conflicts": [
  {
   "topic": {
    "id": "Wujud makhluk",
    "en": "The creature's shape"
   },
   "positions": [

     "summary": {

  },

 "short_description": {
  "id": "Stray sod adalah gumpalan rumput yang disihir peri dalam cerita rakyat; orang yang menginjaknya menjadi bingung dan tersesat sampai sihirnya dipatahkan.",
  "en": "A stray sod is a clump of grass enchanted by faeries in folklore; a person who steps on it becomes disoriented and lost until the enchantment is broken.",
  "claim_ids": [
   "stray-sod-c01",
   "stray-sod-c02",

 "long_description": [
  {
   "id": "Stray sod adalah gumpalan rumput yang disihir oleh peri. Siapa pun yang menginjaknya menjadi bingung dan tersesat, bahkan di tempat yang dikenalnya, dan mengenakan pakaian terbalik dipercaya memutus sihir itu. Konsep dan frasa ini tampaknya berasal dari mitologi Kelt kuno, khususnya cerita rakyat Irlandia.",
   "en": "A stray sod is a clump of grass enchanted by faeries. Anyone who steps on one grows disoriented and lost, even in familiar surroundings, and wearing clothing inside-out is believed to break the spell. The concept and phrase seem to come from ancient Celtic mythology, specifically Irish folklore.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "stray-sod-c01",
    "stray-sod-c02",
    "stray-sod-c03",
    "stray-sod-c04"
   ]
  },
  {
   "id": "Dalam tafsiran modern yang dikristenkan, sumber sihir itu mungkin bukan peri. Catatan dalam arsip Sekolah dúchas.ie menyebut sebuah stray sod di bukit Tom Lee di Derreen tempat beberapa orang tersesat; seorang bernama Mick Winters yang tersesat di sana kemudian duduk dan diajak bermain kartu oleh sekumpulan lelaki kecil berpakaian merah.",
   "en": "In more modern Christianized readings the source of the enchantment may not be fairies. A record in the dúchas.ie Schools' Collection names a stray sod on Tom Lee's hill in Derreen where several people went astray; a man called Mick Winters who lost his way there sat down and was invited to play cards by a crowd of little red men.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "stray-sod-c05",
    "stray-sod-c07",
    "stray-sod-c08",

  {
   "id": "Setelah fajar, lelaki-lelaki kecil itu menghilang dan Mick pulang dengan selamat; ia berkata mungkin ia telah berjalan di jalur peri. Stray sod juga muncul dalam serial The Spiderwick Chronicles, membuat pengelana tersesat.",
   "en": "At dawn the little men vanished and Mick got home safely; he said he must have walked on a fairies' pass. Stray sods also appear in the series The Spiderwick Chronicles, disorienting travellers.",

 "short_description": {
  "id": "Black Dog of Newgate adalah legenda hantu anjing hitam raksasa yang menghantui bekas Penjara Newgate, London, dan dikaitkan dengan kisah penyihir yang dibunuh narapidana saat kelaparan.",
  "en": "The Black Dog of Newgate is a legend of a monstrous black dog haunting the former Newgate Prison in London, tied to the story of a sorcerer killed by inmates during a famine.",
  "claim_ids": [
   "the-black-dog-of-newgate-c01",
   "the-black-dog-of-newgate-c04",
   "the-black-dog-of-newgate-c05"
  ]
 },
 "long_description": [
  {
   "id": "Black Dog of Newgate adalah legenda tentang hantu di bekas Penjara Newgate, London, yang terletak di sebelah Old Bailey. Kisah ini contoh kategori Black Dog Inggris, yaitu anjing pemburu hantu pertanda buruk atau berniat jahat. Catatan tertuanya berasal dari 1596 dan dikreditkan kepada narapidana bernama Luke Hutton.",
   "en": "The Black Dog of Newgate is a legend about a haunting at the former Newgate Prison in London, which stood beside the Old Bailey. It is an example of the English Black Dog type, a spectral hound of ill omen or malicious intent. The earliest recorded account dates from 1596 and is credited to an inmate named Luke Hutton.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "the-black-dog-of-newgate-c01",
    "the-black-dog-of-newgate-c02",
    "the-black-dog-of-newgate-c03"
   ]
  },
  {
   "id": "Dalam ceritanya, saat kelaparan pada masa Raja Henry III dari Inggris, seorang cendekiawan yang dikenal sebagai penyihir dipenjara di Newgate. Para narapidana, yang sudah beralih ke kanibalisme, memakannya. Tak lama kemudian mereka konon melihat anjing hitam raksasa yang dianggap arwah sang penyihir; ia membunuh dan memakan para pelaku satu per satu sampai penyintas terakhir kabur dari penjara.",
   "en": "The tale is set during a famine under England's King Henry III, when a scholar reputed to be a sorcerer was locked up in Newgate. The prisoners, who had already turned to cannibalism, ate him. Soon afterwards they reportedly saw a monstrous black dog taken to be the sorcerer's spirit; it killed and ate those responsible one by one until the last survivors broke out of the prison.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "the-black-dog-of-newgate-c04",
    "the-black-dog-of-newgate-c05",
    "the-black-dog-of-newgate-c06",
    "the-black-dog-of-newgate-c11"
   ]
  },
  {
   "id": "Menjelang akhir narasi, orang asing tak bernama itu mengaku kisah anjing roh tidak benar dan satu-satunya Black Dog yang ia dengar adalah batu hitam besar di ruang bawah tanah. Kisah ini dimaksudkan sebagai cerita moral melawan hidup nista para narapidana.",
   "en": "Towards the end of the narrative the unnamed stranger says the spirit-dog tale is untrue and the only Black Dog he has heard of is a great black stone in the dungeon. The story was meant as a morality tale against the base living of the inmates.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "the-black-dog-of-newgate-c07",
    "the-black-dog-of-newgate-c08"
   ]
  },
  {
   "id": "Ada spekulasi bahwa hantu ini berkaitan dengan Amen Court di dekatnya. Menurut sumber kedua, hantunya muncul sebagai bentuk hitam tak berbentuk yang menyelinap di sepanjang dinding dan mengeluarkan bau menjijikkan, disertai bunyi langkah diseret.",
   "en": "There is speculation that the haunting is linked with nearby Amen Court. The second source reports a ghost in the form of a formless black shape that creeps along walls, gives off a foul smell, and is accompanied by the sound of dragging footsteps.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "the-black-dog-of-newgate-c09",
    "the-black-dog-of-newgate-c12",
    "the-black-dog-of-newgate-c13"
   ]
  }
 ],

  "id": "Putri duyung Staithes adalah dua putri duyung dalam legenda desa Staithes di Yorkshire yang ditawan penduduk lalu mengutuk desa itu dengan nubuat tentang laut dan Jackdaw's Well.",

  {
   "id": "Putri duyung Staithes disebut dalam legenda dari desa Staithes di Yorkshire. Menurut legenda, dua putri duyung naik ke darat dan dijadikan tawanan oleh penduduk. Ketika mencapai laut, salah satunya mengutuk desa dengan nubuat bahwa suatu hari kelak laut akan mengalir ke Jackdaw's Well, sedangkan yang lain berbicara teka-teki tentang egg-broth.",
   "en": "The Mermaids of Staithes appear in a legend from the village of Staithes in Yorkshire. In the legend, two mermaids came ashore and were taken prisoner by the villagers.   },
  {
   "id": "Jackdaw's Well adalah sumber air penting desa, mengandung mineral berkhasiat obat dan pengawet. Egg-broth, air bekas merebus telur, muncul dalam sejumlah dongeng sebagai cara membongkar changeling.",
   "en": "Jackdaw's Well supplied the village with water rich in minerals thought to heal and preserve. Egg-broth, the water left after boiling eggs, turns up in several folktales as a way to expose changelings.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "the-mermaids-of-staithes-c05",
    "the-mermaids-of-staithes-c06"
   ]
  },
  {

Para peneliti menemukan kemiripan dengan cerita putri duyung dari Cornwall, Wales, Skotlandia, dan Pulau Man, menduga motif egg-broth menandakan kisah ini muncul lebih awal, dan menghubungkan kemunculan pertamanya di media cetak dengan banjir pasang berulang dan kemunduran ekonomi Staithes.",
   "en": "The oldest verbal and published accounts on record date from January and March 1924.  "short_description": {
  "id": "Tiddy Mun adalah roh rawa legendaris di Inggris yang dipercaya mengendalikan air dan kabut di rawa Lincolnshire dan Isle of Ely, digambarkan seperti lelaki tua kecil berjanggut putih.",
  "en": "Tiddy Mun was a legendary bog spirit in England believed to control the waters and mists of the Lincolnshire and Isle of Ely fens, described as a small old man with a white beard.",
  "claim_ids": [
   "tiddy-mun-c01",
   "tiddy-mun-c07"
  ]
 },
 "long_description": [
  {
   "id": "Tiddy Mun adalah roh rawa legendaris di Inggris yang dipercaya mampu mengendalikan air dan kabut di rawa-rawa South Lincolnshire, The Carrs di North Lincolnshire, dan rawa Isle of Ely. Kepercayaan ini pertama kali didokumentasikan pada Juni 1891 dalam artikel M. C. Balfour di jurnal Folk-Lore.",
   "en": "Tiddy Mun was a legendary bog spirit of England, thought to hold sway over the water and fog of the South Lincolnshire Fens, the North Lincolnshire Carrs and the Isle of Ely fens. M. C. Balfour put the belief on record for the first time in an article in the journal Folk-Lore, published in June 1891.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "tiddy-mun-c01",
    "tiddy-mun-c02"
   ]
  },
  {
   "id": "Balfour menggambarkannya tidak lebih besar daripada anak tiga tahun, tetapi berpenampilan seperti lelaki tua berambut putih kusut dan berjanggut putih kusut. Ia dikatakan memakai jubah abu-abu sehingga sulit terlihat saat senja, dan tawanya menyerupai panggilan burung peewit. Dalam teks dialek yang dicatat Balfour, ia tinggal di lubang-lubang air rawa, dan penutur lamanya mengaku pernah melihatnya berjalan pincang di kabut, abu-abu dan putih, menjerit seperti peewit.",
   "en": "Balfour describes him as small, no larger than a toddler of three, yet resembling an old man whose long white hair was tangled and whose white beard was matted. A grey gown reportedly made him hard to see at dusk, and his laughter resembled the call of the peewit. In the dialect text Balfour recorded, he dwells in the water-holes of the fen, and the old teller claims to have seen him limping through the fog, grey and white, screeching like a peewit.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "tiddy-mun-c07",
    "tiddy-mun-c08",
    "tiddy-mun-c12",
    "tiddy-mun-c13",
    "tiddy-mun-c14"
   ]
  },
  {
   "id": "Dalam sebuah cerita dari Lembah Ancholme, Tiddy Mun menjatuhkan kutukan wabah pada sebuah desa karena marah atas pengeringan Fens oleh orang Belanda di bawah Cornelius Vermuyden pada abad ke-17. Ia akhirnya reda setelah penduduk berkumpul senja saat bulan baru, menuang ember air ke parit, dan meminta maaf atas kerusakan itu.",
   "en": "In a story from the Ancholme Valley, Tiddy Mun laid a plague-curse on a village, furious that the Dutch under Cornelius Vermuyden were draining the Fens in the seventeenth century. He calmed down in the end once the villagers met at dusk at the new moon, emptied buckets of water into the dyke and said sorry for the harm done.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "tiddy-mun-c03",
    "tiddy-mun-c04",
    "tiddy-mun-c05"
   ]
  },
  {
   "id": "Ia tidak sepenuhnya jahat: bila rawa banjir dan air mencapai desa, orang keluar malam hari dan memanggilnya sampai terdengar teriakan peewit, dan keesokan paginya air surut. Darwin Horn menafsirkan kutukan-kutukannya sebagai kemalangan dan penyakit akibat pengeringan lahan rawa. Penutur dialek menduga Tiddy Mun telah ketakutan dan pergi oleh cara-cara baru sehingga orang tak lagi mengenalnya.",
   "en": "He was not exclusively malevolent: when the fen flooded and the waters reached the villages, people went out at night and called him until they heard the cry of a peewit, and by morning the waters had receded. Darwin Horn reads his curses as the misfortune and disease brought by draining the fenland. The dialect teller guesses he has been frightened away by new ways, so that people no longer know him.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "tiddy-mun-c06",
    "tiddy-mun-c11",
    "tiddy-mun-c16"
   ]
  }
 ],
