 ],
 "claims": [
  {
   "id": "tiddy-mun-c01",
   "source_id": "tiddy-mun-s1",
   "quote": "Tiddy Mun was a legendary bog spirit in England, who was believed to have the ability to control the waters and mists of The Fens of South Lincolnshire, The Carrs of North Lincolnshire and fens of the Isle of Ely.",
   "locator": "Intro",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Tiddy Mun adalah roh rawa legendaris di Inggris yang dipercaya mampu mengendalikan air dan kabut di rawa-rawa South Lincolnshire, The Carrs di North Lincolnshire, dan rawa Isle of Ely.",
    "en": "Tiddy Mun was a legendary bog spirit in England believed able to control the waters and mists of the Fens of South Lincolnshire, the Carrs of North Lincolnshire and the fens of the Isle of Ely."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "tiddy-mun-c02",
   "source_id": "tiddy-mun-s1",
   "quote": "The belief in Tiddy Mun was first documented in June 1891 in an article by M. C. Balfour in the Folklore Society journal Folk-Lore.",
   "locator": "Legend",
   "context": "historical-record",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Kepercayaan akan Tiddy Mun pertama kali didokumentasikan pada Juni 1891 dalam artikel M. C. Balfour di jurnal Folklore Society, Folk-Lore.",
    "en": "The belief in Tiddy Mun was first documented in June 1891 in an article by M. C. Balfour in the Folklore Society journal Folk-Lore."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "tiddy-mun-c03",
   "source_id": "tiddy-mun-s1",
   "quote": "In the article she recalls a story, collected in the Ancholme Valley, told to her by an older person",
   "locator": "Legend",
   "context": "historical-record",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Dalam artikel itu Balfour mengisahkan cerita yang dikumpulkan di Lembah Ancholme dan diceritakan oleh seorang yang lebih tua.",
    "en": "In the article she recalls a story, collected in the Ancholme Valley, told to her by an older person."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "tiddy-mun-c04",
   "source_id": "tiddy-mun-s1",
   "quote": "a curse of pestilence that had been cast upon his village by the Tiddy Mun, who was angered at the draining of the Fens by the Dutch, led by Cornelius Vermuyden, in the seventeenth century.",
   "locator": "Legend",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Dalam cerita itu, Tiddy Mun menjatuhkan kutukan wabah pada sebuah desa karena marah atas pengeringan Fens oleh orang Belanda di bawah Cornelius Vermuyden pada abad ke-17.",
    "en": "In the story Tiddy Mun cast a curse of pestilence upon a village, angered at the draining of the Fens by the Dutch led by Cornelius Vermuyden in the seventeenth century."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "tiddy-mun-c05",
   "source_id": "tiddy-mun-s1",
   "quote": "According to the story the Tiddy Mun was eventually placated after the villagers gathered at twilight at the time of the new moon, poured buckets of water into the dyke and apologised for the damage caused.",
   "locator": "Legend",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Menurut cerita, Tiddy Mun akhirnya reda setelah penduduk berkumpul senja saat bulan baru, menuang ember air ke parit, dan meminta maaf atas kerusakan yang terjadi.",
    "en": "According to the story Tiddy Mun was eventually placated after the villagers gathered at twilight at the new moon, poured buckets of water into the dyke and apologised for the damage caused."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "tiddy-mun-c06",
   "source_id": "tiddy-mun-s1",
   "quote": "He was not exclusively malevolent; if the Fens flooded and the waters reached the villages, people would go out at night and call Tiddy Mun",
   "locator": "Legend",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Ia tidak sepenuhnya jahat: bila Fens banjir dan air mencapai desa, orang keluar malam hari dan memanggil Tiddy Mun.",
    "en": "He was not exclusively malevolent: if the Fens flooded and the waters reached the villages, people would go out at night and call Tiddy Mun."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "tiddy-mun-c07",
   "source_id": "tiddy-mun-s1",
   "quote": "The Tiddy Mun was described, by folklorist M.C. Balfour in 1891, as being no bigger than a three-year-old child, but looking like an old man with long, tangled white hair and a matted white beard.",
   "locator": "Description",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Balfour pada 1891 menggambarkan Tiddy Mun tidak lebih besar daripada anak tiga tahun tetapi berpenampilan seperti lelaki tua berambut putih panjang kusut dan berjanggut putih kusut.",
    "en": "Balfour in 1891 described Tiddy Mun as no bigger than a three-year-old child but looking like an old man with long, tangled white hair and a matted white beard."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "tiddy-mun-c08",
   "source_id": "tiddy-mun-s1",
   "quote": "He is said to have worn a grey gown so that at dusk he was difficult to see. His laughter was said to resemble the call of the peewit.",
   "locator": "Description",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Ia dikatakan mengenakan jubah abu-abu sehingga sulit terlihat saat senja, dan tawanya konon menyerupai panggilan burung peewit.",
    "en": "He is said to have worn a grey gown so that at dusk he was difficult to see, and his laughter was said to resemble the call of the peewit."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "tiddy-mun-c09",
   "source_id": "tiddy-mun-s1",
   "quote": "Writing in 1955, folklorist E. H. Rudkin also records another Ancholme Valley belief of an imp-like race of beings who were generally considered mischievous but benevolent.",
   "locator": "The Tiddy people",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Pada 1955 E. H. Rudkin mencatat kepercayaan Lembah Ancholme lain tentang ras makhluk mirip imp yang umumnya dianggap nakal tetapi baik hati.",
    "en": "Writing in 1955, E. H. Rudkin records another Ancholme Valley belief in an imp-like race generally considered mischievous but benevolent."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "tiddy-mun-c10",
   "source_id": "tiddy-mun-s1",
   "quote": "The Tiddy people would dance, by moonlight, on large flat stones, known as Strangers Stones, found in the area.",
   "locator": "The Tiddy people",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Orang Tiddy menari di bawah cahaya bulan di atas batu datar besar yang disebut Strangers Stones.",
    "en": "The Tiddy people would dance by moonlight on large flat stones known as Strangers Stones."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "tiddy-mun-c11",
   "source_id": "tiddy-mun-s1",
   "quote": "may be connected to misfortune and disease brought about by the effect of draining the fenland.",
   "locator": "Legend",
   "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Darwin Horn berpendapat kutukan-kutukan Tiddy Mun dapat dikaitkan dengan kemalangan dan penyakit akibat pengeringan lahan rawa.",
    "en": "Darwin Horn argues that Tiddy Mun's curses may be connected to misfortune and disease brought about by the draining of the fenland."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "tiddy-mun-c12",
   "source_id": "tiddy-mun-s2",
   "quote": "Tiddy Mun dwelt i' tha watter-holes, an' noo tha Dutchies wor a emptyin' 'em out",
   "locator": "Legends of the Cars, Tiddy Mun",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Dalam teks dialek Balfour, Tiddy Mun tinggal di lubang-lubang air (watter-holes), dan orang Belanda (Dutchies) sedang mengosongkannya.",
    "en": "In Balfour's dialect text Tiddy Mun dwelt in the water-holes (watter-holes), and the Dutch (Dutchies) were emptying them out."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "tiddy-mun-c13",
   "source_id": "tiddy-mun-s2",
   "quote": "for a wor none bigger 'n a three year's bairn, but a hadn't rightly no sort of a name",
   "locator": "Legends of the Cars, Tiddy Mun",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Ia disebut Tiddy Mun karena tidak lebih besar daripada anak berumur tiga tahun (three year's bairn), tetapi sebenarnya tidak punya nama.",
    "en": "He was called Tiddy Mun because he was none bigger than a three-year-old child (three year's bairn), but had no real name."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "tiddy-mun-c14",
   "source_id": "tiddy-mun-s2",
   "quote": "a've seen un mysel, limpin' by i' tha fog, all grey an' white an' screechin' like tha pyewipe",
   "locator": "Legends of the Cars, Tiddy Mun",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Si penutur lama mengaku pernah melihatnya berjalan pincang di kabut, abu-abu dan putih, menjerit seperti burung pyewipe (peewit).",
    "en": "The old teller claims to have seen him limping by in the fog, all grey and white and screeching like the pyewipe (peewit)."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "tiddy-mun-c15",
   "source_id": "tiddy-mun-s2",
   "quote": "Tiddy Mun wi-out a name Here's watter for thee !",
   "locator": "Legends of the Cars, Tiddy Mun",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Setiap bulan baru orang menuang air ke parit sambil berseru kepada Tiddy Mun tanpa nama: \"Here's watter for thee!\" (ini air untukmu).",
    "en": "At every new moon people poured water into the dyke crying to Tiddy Mun without a name: \"Here's watter for thee!\" (here is water for thee)."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "tiddy-mun-c16",
   "source_id": "tiddy-mun-s2",
   "quote": "Tiddy Mun 's bin' frighted away wi' a' tha new ways an' gear, for folk dinna ken un no more",
   "locator": "Legends of the Cars, Tiddy Mun",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Penutur menduga Tiddy Mun telah ketakutan dan pergi oleh cara-cara dan perkakas baru sehingga orang tak lagi mengenalnya.",
    "en": "The teller guesses Tiddy Mun has been frightened away by all the new ways and gear, so folk no longer know him."
   }
  }
 ],
 "conflicts": [],
 "gaps": [

 "short_description": {
  "id": "Yallery Brown adalah roh alam jahil mirip peri dalam dongeng Lincolnshire, Inggris: makhluk mungil berkulit cokelat yang dibebaskan dari bawah batu oleh seorang pemuda lalu menjadi pembawa sial bagi pemuda itu.",
  "en": "Yallery Brown is a mischievous fairy-like nature spirit in a Lincolnshire folk tale from England: a tiny brown creature freed from under a stone by a young man who then turns his luck sour.",
  "claim_ids": [
   "yallery-brown-c01",

   "yallery-brown-c08",

 "long_description": [
  {
   "id": "Yallery Brown adalah roh alam jahil mirip peri dalam dongeng lama Lincolnshire di Inggris. Kisahnya pertama kali dikutip M. C. Balfour pada 1891 dari cerita yang dikumpulkan di Lembah Ancholme, North Lincolnshire, lalu juga dimuat Joseph Jacobs dalam More English Fairy Tales.",
   "en": "The tale of Yallery Brown, a mischievous fairy-like nature spirit, is an old Lincolnshire folk story from England. M. C. Balfour first cited the story in 1891 from tales collected in the Ancholme Valley, North Lincolnshire, and Joseph Jacobs later included it in More English Fairy Tales.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "yallery-brown-c01",
    "yallery-brown-c02",
    "yallery-brown-c03"
   ]
  },
  {
   "id": "Dalam versi Jacobs, pemuda bernama Tom Tiver mengangkat batu pipih dan menemukan makhluk mungil telentang di lubang. Rambut dan janggutnya panjang dan kusut melilit tubuhnya, dan kulitnya cokelat seperti tanah yang baru dibalik. Ia menyebut dirinya Yallery Brown dan menawarkan bantuan, dengan syarat Tom tak boleh berterima kasih.",
   "en": "In Jacobs's version the lad Tom Tiver lifts a flat stone and finds a tiny creature on its back in the hole. Its hair and beard are long and matted around its body, and its skin is brown like freshly turned earth. He calls himself Yallery Brown and offers help, on condition that Tom must never thank him.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "yallery-brown-c05",
    "yallery-brown-c06",
    "yallery-brown-c07",
    "yallery-brown-c08",
    "yallery-brown-c09",
    "yallery-brown-c10"
   ]
  },
  {
   "id": "Pekerjaan Tom selesai dengan sendirinya, tetapi pekerjaan rekan-rekannya dirusak dan Tom tidak bisa mengerjakan apa pun sendiri. Yallery Brown hanya terlihat dalam gelap. Ketika Tom akhirnya berterima kasih, makhluk itu menyatakan ia tidak akan membantu lagi dan tidak akan pernah meninggalkan Tom; kesialan menimpa Tom seumur hidupnya.",
   "en": "Tom's work gets done by itself, but his fellows' work is undone and Tom can do nothing on his own. Yallery Brown is seen only in the dark. When Tom finally thanks him, the creature declares he will help no more and will never leave Tom alone; misfortune dogs Tom for life.",

   "summary": {
    "id": "Tom membebaskan makhluk mungil dari bawah batu; makhluk itu menolongnya bekerja tetapi merusak pekerjaan orang lain, dan setelah Tom berterima kasih ia menimpakan kesialan seumur hidup.",
    "en": "Tom frees a tiny creature from under a stone; the creature helps him work but ruins others' work, and after Tom thanks him he brings lifelong misfortune."

 "claims": [
  {
   "id": "yallery-brown-c01",
   "source_id": "yallery-brown-s1",
   "quote": "Yallery Brown is a mischievous fairy-like nature spirit in an old Lincolnshire folk tale from England, which itself is usually named after the creature.",
   "locator": "Intro",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Yallery Brown adalah roh alam jahil mirip peri dalam dongeng lama Lincolnshire di Inggris, yang dongengnya sendiri biasanya dinamai menurut makhluk itu.",
    "en": "Yallery Brown is a mischievous fairy-like nature spirit in an old Lincolnshire folk tale from England, which is itself usually named after the creature."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "yallery-brown-c02",
   "source_id": "yallery-brown-s1",
   "quote": "This story was first cited by M C Balfour in 1891 within a set of stories collected in the Ancholme Valley of North Lincolnshire, and published in the Folklore Society journal Folklore",
   "locator": "In Collections",
   "context": "historical-record",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Kisah ini pertama kali dikutip M. C. Balfour pada 1891 dalam kumpulan cerita dari Lembah Ancholme di North Lincolnshire, dan terbit di jurnal Folklore Society, Folklore.",
    "en": "The story was first cited by M. C. Balfour in 1891 within a set of stories collected in the Ancholme Valley of North Lincolnshire and published in the Folklore Society journal Folklore."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "yallery-brown-c03",
   "source_id": "yallery-brown-s1",
   "quote": "The story was also sent to Joseph Jacobs and included in his More English Fairy tales.",
   "locator": "In Collections",
   "context": "historical-record",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Kisah itu juga dikirim kepada Joseph Jacobs dan dimuat dalam More English Fairy Tales.",
    "en": "The story was also sent to Joseph Jacobs and included in his More English Fairy Tales."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "yallery-brown-c04",
   "source_id": "yallery-brown-s1",
   "quote": "Balfour's tale has the teller speaking in first person.",
   "locator": "In Collections",
   "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Dalam versi Balfour, penutur bercerita dengan sudut pandang orang pertama.",
    "en": "In Balfour's version the teller speaks in the first person."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "yallery-brown-c05",
   "source_id": "yallery-brown-s2",
   "quote": "there was a young lad of eighteen or so named Tom Tiver working on the Hall Farm.",
   "locator": "Yallery Brown",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Dalam versi Jacobs, ada seorang pemuda delapan belas tahun bernama Tom Tiver yang bekerja di Hall Farm.",
    "en": "In Jacobs's version, a young lad of eighteen or so named Tom Tiver worked on the Hall Farm."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "yallery-brown-c06",
   "source_id": "yallery-brown-s2",
   "quote": "And there in the hole lay a tiddy thing on its back, blinking up at the moon and at him.",
   "locator": "Yallery Brown",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Setelah batu diangkat, di dalam lubang terbaring makhluk mungil (tiddy thing) telentang, berkedip menatap bulan dan Tom.",
    "en": "After the stone is lifted, a tiddy thing lies on its back in the hole, blinking up at the moon and at Tom."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "yallery-brown-c07",
   "source_id": "yallery-brown-s2",
   "quote": "but it had long cotted hair and beard, twisted round and round its body so that you couldn't see its clothes",
   "locator": "Yallery Brown",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Makhluk itu berambut dan berjanggut panjang kusut yang melilit tubuhnya sehingga pakaiannya tak terlihat.",
    "en": "It had long matted hair and beard twisted round its body so that its clothes could not be seen."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "yallery-brown-c08",
   "source_id": "yallery-brown-s2",
   "quote": "the skin was the colour of the fresh turned earth in the spring—brown as brown could be",
   "locator": "Yallery Brown",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Kulitnya berwarna seperti tanah yang baru dibalik di musim semi, cokelat sekali.",
    "en": "Its skin was the colour of freshly turned earth in spring, brown as brown could be."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "yallery-brown-c09",
   "source_id": "yallery-brown-s2",
   "quote": "Yallery Brown thou mayst call me, Yallery Brown; 't is my nature seest thou, and as for a name 't will do as any other.",
   "locator": "Yallery Brown",
   "context": "etymology",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Makhluk itu sendiri menyuruh Tom menyebutnya Yallery Brown, \"karena begitulah sifatku\", dan menyebut nama itu cukup sebagai nama seperti yang lain.",
    "en": "The creature himself tells Tom to call him Yallery Brown, \"'t is my nature\", and says it will do as a name like any other."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "yallery-brown-c10",
   "source_id": "yallery-brown-s2",
   "quote": "I'll help thee and welcome, but if ever thou sayest that to me—if ever thou thankest me, see'st thou, thou 'lt never see me more.",
   "locator": "Yallery Brown",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Yallery Brown bersedia membantu, tetapi bila Tom pernah berterima kasih kepadanya, Tom tidak akan melihatnya lagi.",
    "en": "Yallery Brown agrees to help but warns that if Tom ever thanks him, Tom will never see him more."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "yallery-brown-c11",
   "source_id": "yallery-brown-s2",
   "quote": "And so it went on day after day, all the work done by Yallery Brown, and better done, too, than he could have done it himself.",
   "locator": "Yallery Brown",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Hari demi hari semua pekerjaan Tom dikerjakan Yallery Brown, bahkan lebih baik daripada jika Tom mengerjakannya sendiri.",
    "en": "Day after day all Tom's work was done by Yallery Brown, and better than Tom could have done it himself."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "yallery-brown-c12",
   "source_id": "yallery-brown-s2",
   "quote": "If the work was done for Tom, 't was undone for the other lads; if his buckets were filled, theirs were upset",
   "locator": "Yallery Brown",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Bila pekerjaan Tom dikerjakan, pekerjaan rekan-rekannya dirusak: ember Tom terisi, ember mereka ditumpahkan.",
    "en": "If Tom's work was done for him, the other lads' work was undone; his buckets were filled while theirs were upset."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "yallery-brown-c13",
   "source_id": "yallery-brown-s2",
   "quote": "For he never saw Yallery Brown in daylight; only in the darklins he saw him hopping about, like a Will-o-th'-wyke without his lanthorn.",
   "locator": "Yallery Brown",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Tom tidak pernah melihat Yallery Brown pada siang hari; hanya dalam gelap ia melihatnya melompat-lompat seperti Will-o-th'-wyke tanpa lenteranya.",
    "en": "Tom never saw Yallery Brown in daylight; only in the dark did he see him hopping about like a Will-o-th'-wyke without his lantern."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "yallery-brown-c14",
   "source_id": "yallery-brown-s2",
   "quote": "I was nice and safe under the stone, Tom, and could do no harm; but thou let me out thyself, and thou can't put me back again!",
   "locator": "Yallery Brown",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Setelah Tom berterima kasih, makhluk itu berkata ia dulu aman di bawah batu dan tak bisa mencelakai, tetapi Tom sendiri yang melepaskannya dan tak bisa menyimpannya kembali.",
    "en": "After Tom thanks him, the creature says he was safe under the stone and could do no harm, but Tom let him out himself and cannot put him back."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "yallery-brown-c15",
   "source_id": "yallery-brown-s2",
   "quote": "And the children died, and the crops rotted—the beasts never fatted, and nothing ever did well with him",
   "locator": "Yallery Brown",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Kutukan itu terwujud: anak-anak meninggal, tanaman membusuk, ternak tak pernah gemuk, dan tak ada yang berhasil bagi Tom.",
    "en": "The curse came true: the children died, the crops rotted, the beasts never fattened, and nothing ever did well with Tom."
   }
  }
 ],
